=== PROMPT VARIANT ===
r1

=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
There are 2N points P_1,P_2,\ldots,P_N, Q_1,Q_2,\ldots,Q_N on a two-dimensional plane.
The coordinates of P_i are (A_i, B_i), and the coordinates of Q_i are (C_i, D_i).
No three different points lie on the same straight line.
Determine whether there exists a permutation R = (R_1, R_2, \ldots, R_N) of (1, 2, \ldots, N) that satisfies the following condition. If such an R exists, find one.

- For each integer i from 1 through N, let segment i be the line segment connecting P_i and Q_{R_i}.  Then, segment i and segment j (1 \leq  i < j \leq N) never intersect.

Input

The input is given from Standard Input in the following format:
N
A_1 B_1
A_2 B_2
\vdots 
A_N B_N
C_1 D_1
C_2 D_2
\vdots
C_N D_N

Output

If there is no R satisfying the condition, print -1.
If such an R exists, print R_1, R_2, \ldots, R_N separated by spaces. If there are multiple solutions, you may print any of them.

Constraints


- 1 \leq N \leq 300
- 0 \leq A_i, B_i, C_i, D_i \leq 5000 (1 \leq i \leq N)
- (A_i, B_i) \neq (A_j, B_j) (1 \leq i < j \leq N)
- (C_i, D_i) \neq (C_j, D_j) (1 \leq i < j \leq N)
- (A_i, B_i) \neq (C_j, D_j) (1 \leq i, j \leq N)
- No three different points lie on the same straight line.
- All input values are integers.

Sample Input 1

3
0 0
2 4
4 2
0 2
2 0
4 4

Sample Output 1

2 1 3

The points are arranged as shown in the following figure.

By setting R = (2, 1, 3), the three line segments do not cross each other. Also, any of R = (1, 2, 3), (1, 3, 2), (2, 3, 1), and (3, 1, 2) is a valid answer.

Sample Input 2

8
59 85
60 57
72 12
3 27
16 58
41 94
77 64
97 20
32 37
7 2
57 94
35 70
38 60
97 100
5 76
38 8

Sample Output 2

3 5 8 2 7 4 6 1

Reason in a commit-implement-recheck way.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

Let's think of "minimum Euclidean matching" again. It is conceptually simple and robust enough. But need code. Let's evaluate Hungarian with floats.

Hungarian algorithm for min cost assignment:
Given n x n cost matrix a[i][j] (floats). Standard O(n^3):
u = [0]*(n+1), v=[0]*(n+1), p=[0]*(n+1), way=[0]*(n+1)
for i in 1..n:
    p[0]=i
    j0=0
    minv=[inf]*(n+1)
    used=[False]*(n+1)
    while True:
        used[j0]=True
        i0=p[j0]
        delta=inf
        j1=0
        for j in 1..n:
            if not used[j]:
                cur = a[i0-1][j-1] - u[i0] - v[j]
                if cur < minv[j]:
                    minv[j]=cur
                    way[j]=j0
                if minv[j] < delta:
                    delta=minv[j]
                    j1=j
        for j in 0..n:
            if used[j]:
                u[p[j]] += delta
                v[j] -= delta
            else:
                minv[j] -= delta
        j0=j1
        if p[j0] == 0:
            break
    while True:
        j1=way[j0]
        p[j0]=p[j1]
        j0=j1
        if j0==0: break
Then assignment: for j in 1..n: ans[p[j]]=j.

This works with floats. Need ensure costs nonnegative. We can use math.hypot for Euclidean distance. Complexity O(n^3) with Python loops: n=300, inner loop 27 million, plus updates 27 million. Fine. Coordinates integer, distances often irrational; ties possible due symmetry. Hungarian can handle ties. If due precision, it might not find exact optimum but likely still noncrossing? Need maybe verify and if crossing, we can "uncross" locally to fix. Since any non-optimal crossing can be uncrossed reducing Euclidean length; if Hungarian output has crossings, we can iteratively uncross pairs until noncrossing. This is a good safety net! We can compute a matching (any) and then while there are crossing edges, swap their partners to the alternative noncrossing pairing, reducing total length. This will terminate because length decreases (strictly) and finite matchings. This is the "uncrossing" algorithm. Actually we can start with any perfect matching (e.g., identity) and repeatedly uncross crossing pairs. Does this always terminate at noncrossing? Yes, each uncrossing reduces total Euclidean length by triangle inequality. Number of matchings finite, so terminates. But could take many swaps? N=300, worst-case maybe O(N^2) swaps? Need analyze. Could be exponential? Each swap reduces length, but length has finite possible? Euclidean sums from sqrt ints not finite? There are finite matchings, so at most N! but could be huge. But in practice maybe okay? Need not rely.

Let's verify theorem: Any minimum Euclidean perfect matching is noncrossing. Proof: Suppose two segments AB and CD cross, where A,C are red? Let's label endpoints of two matched pairs: P1-Q1 and P2-Q2. They cross. The alternative matching P1-Q2 and P2-Q1 has total length strictly less. Need prove with triangle inequality. Let segments AC and BD cross at O. In convex quadrilateral with vertices A,B,C,D in order, the crossing segments are AC and BD. Alternative matching is AD and BC (assuming colors allow; since A,C are one red-blue pair and B,D another, the colors around quadrilateral are red, red, blue, blue? Let's check if A,C are endpoints of segment1 and B,D of segment2. Around order A,B,C,D, colors: A red, B red, C blue, D blue (if P1=A red, Q1=C blue; P2=B red, Q2=D blue). Alternative AD (red-blue) and BC (red-blue). Need show AD+BC < AC+BD. Since O lies on AC and BD. By triangle inequality:
AD < AO + OD
BC < BO + OC
Sum < AO+OD+BO+OC = (AO+OC)+(BO+OD) = AC+BD.
Yes! Strict because O not on AD or BC (no collinear? O could be on AD? If A,O,D collinear? So strict. Good. Thus any crossing pair can be uncrossed to strictly reduce total Euclidean length. Therefore min matching cannot cross.

Now, Hungarian algorithm details.

Let's write code:

import sys, math

def hungarian(a):
    n = len(a)
    u = [0.0]*(n+1)
    v = [0.0]*(n+1)
    p = [0]*(n+1)
    way = [0]*(n+1)
    for i in range(1, n+1):
        p[0] = i
        j0 = 0
        minv = [math.inf]*(n+1)
        used = [False]*(n+1)
        while True:
            used[j0] = True
            i0 = p[j0]
            delta = math.inf
            j1 = 0
            row = a[i0-1]
            ui0 = u[i0]
            for j in range(1, n+1):
                if not used[j]:
                    cur = row[j-1] - ui0 - v[j]
                    if cur < minv[j]:
                        minv[j] = cur
                        way[j] = j0
                    if minv[j] < delta:
                        delta = minv[j]
                        j1 = j
            for j in range(0, n+1):
                if used[j]:
                    u[p[j]] += delta
                    v[j] -= delta
                else:
                    minv[j] -= delta
            j0 = j1
            if p[j0] == 0:
                break
        while True:
            j1 = way[j0]
            p[j0] = p[j1]
            j0 = j1
            if j0 == 0:
                break
    ans = [0]*n
    for j in range(1, n+1):
        if p[j] != 0:
            ans[p[j]-1] = j
    return ans

Potential issue: In update loop, for j=0 used, p[0]=i, u[i] += delta. For j used columns, p[j] is row assigned to that column; update u[p[j]]. For j=0, p[0]=i. Good. But if p[j]=0 for some used? used columns are in alternating tree; p[j] should be nonzero except j0? Wait j0 is current column, may be unmatched? Actually in the while, j0 is a column that is in tree. At the start of iteration, j0 is the column we are visiting. It may be unmatched (p[j0]==0) only when we break after updating? Let's recall algorithm: The loop breaks when p[j0]==0 after setting j0=j1. At that point, before breaking, we have updated potentials and j0 is an unmatched column. We do not go to next iteration, so we never call used[j0] on unmatched? Actually after break, we augment. In the update loop before break, j0 (new) is not used yet, so minv[j0] was updated. Then break. So in the next iteration, used[j0] would be set if not break. Since break, no issue. In update loop, used[j] for j columns in tree all have p[j] nonzero? The root column 0 has p[0]=i nonzero. Other used columns are matched? In Hungarian, when we visit a column j0, if p[j0]==0 we break before marking used? Actually standard code marks used[j0]=True at start, then after scanning and updating, sets j0=j1; if p[j0]==0 break. So the unmatched column is not marked used. Thus used columns all have p[j] nonzero. Good.

But in update loop, for j used, u[p[j]] += delta. If p[j]=0? not. For j=0, p[0]=i nonzero. Good.

Potential issue with `minv[j] -= delta` for j=0? j=0 used, so else not. For j unused, minv[j] is finite after first scan? At first iteration, all unused columns get finite cur. So okay. If some minv[j] remains inf? No.

Now, if costs are floats, `delta` can be 0. Then update no change, but algorithm still progresses? It may cycle if delta=0 and j1? In Hungarian, delta can be 0 if there are ties; the algorithm still changes matching via augmenting path and terminates because each augmentation increases number of matched columns. Even with zero delta, the alternating tree grows (used set increases) each iteration, so no infinite loop. Good.

How to detect segment intersection efficiently? N=300, O(N^2)=90k, fine. For each pair i<j, check if segment P_i-Q_{ans[i]} and P_j-Q_{ans[j]} intersect properly (including endpoints? Points distinct, segments could share endpoints? No, P_i distinct from Q_j, and each P matched to unique Q; two segments have distinct P and distinct Q endpoints, so no shared endpoints. Could they intersect at an endpoint of one lying on the other? That would be three points collinear? Since endpoint is a point from set, if it lies on segment between two other points, then three points collinear, prohibited. So only proper crossing. Need orientation.

Use orientation cross product. For segments AB and CD, proper intersection if orientations (A,B,C) and (A,B,D) have opposite signs and (C,D,A) and (C,D,B) have opposite signs. Since no collinear, strict.

If crossing, swap ans[i] and ans[j]? Let's see: Current matching has P_i-Q_a and P_j-Q_b crossing. Alternative matching is P_i-Q_b and P_j-Q_a. So swap the Q indices for i and j. This reduces Euclidean length. Continue until no crossings. We can implement a loop:

def orient(a,b,c): return (b[0]-a[0])*(c[1]-a[1]) - (b[1]-a[1])*(c[0]-a[0])
def intersect(i,j):
    a=P[i]; b=Q[ans[i]]; c=P[j]; d=Q[ans[j]]
    return orient(a,b,c)*orient(a,b,d)<0 and orient(c,d,a)*orient(c,d,b)<0

while True:
    found=False
    for i in range(N):
        for j in range(i+1,N):
            if intersect(i,j):
                ans[i], ans[j] = ans[j], ans[i]
                found=True
                break
        if found: break
    if not found: break

This could be O(N^2) per swap; if many swaps maybe. But likely none after Hungarian. We can include verification only, maybe if crossing, swap and restart. If Hungarian correct, no swaps. If due numerical, maybe few. But if Hungarian has a bug causing many crossings, could be slow. But okay.

Now, let's examine Hungarian algorithm correctness with real costs and potential initialization. The standard algorithm for rectangular assignment (n rows, n cols) with costs `a[i][j]`:
```
u = [0]*(n+1)
v = [0]*(n+1)
p = [0]*(n+1)
way = [0]*(n+1)
for i in 1..n:
    p[0] = i
    j0 = 0
    minv = [inf]*(n+1)
    used = [False]*(n+1)
    while True:
        used[j0] = True
        i0 = p[j0]
        delta = inf
        j1 = 0
        for j in 1..n:
            if not used[j]:
                cur = a[i0][j] - u[i0] - v[j]
                if cur < minv[j]:
                    minv[j] = cur
                    way[j] = j0
                if minv[j] < delta:
                    delta = minv[j]
                    j1 = j
        for j in 0..n:
            if used[j]:
                u[p[j]] += delta
                v[j] -= delta
            else:
                minv[j] -= delta
        j0 = j1
        if p[j0] == 0:
            break
    while True:
        j1 = way[j0]
        p[j0] = p[j1]
        j0 = j1
        if j0 == 0:
            break
```

    c=P[j]; d=Q[ans[j]]

```
def hungarian(a):
    n = len(a)
    u = [0.0]*(n+1)
    v = [0.0]*(n+1)
    p = [0]*(n+1)
    way = [0]*(n+1)
    for i in range(1, n+1):
        p[0] = i
        j0 = 0
        minv = [inf]*(n+1)
        used = [False]*(n+1)
        while True:
            used[j0] = True
            i0 = p[j0]
            row = a[i0-1]
            ui = u[i0]
            delta = inf
            j1 = 0
            for j in range(1, n+1):
                if not used[j]:
                    cur = row[j-1] - ui - v[j]
                    if cur < minv[j]:
                        minv[j] = cur
                        way[j] = j0
                    if minv[j] < delta:
                        delta = minv[j]
                        j1 = j
            for j in range(0, n+1):
                if used[j]:
                    u[p[j]] += delta
                    v[j] -= delta
                else:
                    minv[j] -= delta
            j0 = j1
            if p[j0] == 0:
                break
        while True:
            j1 = way[j0]
            p[j0] = p[j1]
            j0 = j1
            if j0 == 0:
                break
    ans = [0]*n
    for j in range(1, n+1):
        ans[p[j]-1] = j
    return ans
```
Potential micro-optimization: In update loop, `u[p[j]] += delta` for j used; if j=0, p[0]=i. For used columns, p[j] nonzero. Good.

```python
import sys, math

def hungarian(a):
    n = len(a)
    u = [0.0]*(n+1)
    v = [0.0]*(n+1)
    p = [0]*(n+1)
    way = [0]*(n+1)
    inf = math.inf
    for i in range(1, n+1):
        p[0] = i
        j0 = 0
        minv = [inf]*(n+1)
        used = [False]*(n+1)
        while True:
            used[j0] = True
            i0 = p[j0]
            row = a[i0-1]
            ui = u[i0]
            delta = inf
            j1 = 0
            for j in range(1, n+1):
                if not used[j]:
                    cur = row[j-1] - ui - v[j]
                    if cur < minv[j]:
                        minv[j] = cur
                        way[j] = j0
                    if minv[j] < delta:
                        delta = minv[j]
                        j1 = j
            for j in range(n+1):
                if used[j]:
                    u[p[j]] += delta
                    v[j] -= delta
                else:
                    minv[j] -= delta
            j0 = j1
            if p[j0] == 0:
                break
        while True:
            j1 = way[j0]
            p[j0] = p[j1]
            j0 = j1
            if j0 == 0:
                break
    ans = [0]*n
    for j in range(1, n+1):
        ans[p[j]-1] = j
    return ans

def main():
    data = sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()
    if not data: return
    it = iter(data)
    N = int(next(it))
    P = [(int(next(it)), int(next(it))) for _ in range(N)]
    Q = [(int(next(it)), int(next(it))) for _ in range(N)]
    cost = [[0.0]*N for _ in range(N)]
    for i in range(N):
        xi, yi = P[i]
        row = cost[i]
        for j in range(N):
            xj, yj = Q[j]
            dx = xi - xj
            dy = yi - yj
            row[j] = math.sqrt(dx*dx + dy*dy)
    ans = hungarian(cost)
    # Uncrossing safety
    def orient(a,b,c):
        return (b[0]-a[0])*(c[1]-a[1]) - (b[1]-a[1])*(c[0]-a[0])
    while True:
        found = False
        for i in range(N):
            xi, yi = P[i]
            qi = ans[i]
            xa, ya = Q[qi]
            for j in range(i+1, N):
                xj, yj = P[j]
                qj = ans[j]
                xb, yb = Q[qj]
                # segment i: (xi,yi)-(xa,ya), segment j: (xj,yj)-(xb,yb)
                o1 = (xa-xi)*(yj-ya) - (ya-yi)*(xj-xa)  # orient P_i-Q_i, P_j? Wait need consistent
```

```

    ans[i], ans[j] = ans[j], ans[i]

Let's write a slightly optimized Hungarian:

```
def hungarian(a):
    n = len(a)
    u = [0.0]*(n+1)
    v = [0.0]*(n+1)
    p = [0]*(n+1)
    way = [0]*(n+1)
    inf = math.inf
    for i in range(1, n+1):
        p[0] = i
        j0 = 0
        minv = [inf]*(n+1)
        used = [False]*(n+1)
        while True:
            used[j0] = True
            i0 = p[j0]
            row = a[i0-1]
            ui = u[i0]
            delta = inf
            j1 = 0
            for j in range(1, n+1):
                if not used[j]:
                    cur = row[j-1] - ui - v[j]
                    if cur < minv[j]:
                        minv[j] = cur
                        way[j] = j0
                    if minv[j] < delta:
                        delta = minv[j]
                        j1 = j
            for j in range(n+1):
                if used[j]:
                    u[p[j]] += delta
                    v[j] -= delta
                else:
                    minv[j] -= delta
            j0 = j1
            if p[j0] == 0:
                break
        while True:
            j1 = way[j0]
            p[j0] = p[j1]
            j0 = j1
            if j0 == 0:
                break
    ans = [0]*n
    for j in range(1, n+1):
        ans[p[j]-1] = j
    return ans
```

There is a variant where you update potentials only at the end using distances from Dijkstra. Let's recall.

The Hungarian algorithm can be implemented as:
For each row i, find shortest augmenting path using potentials. The potentials u/v are updated after each augmentation, not during Dijkstra? Let's derive.

Standard algorithm above updates potentials inside the Dijkstra loop (when scanning a new column). This is the "Hungarian" with potentials. Another implementation:
```
for i in 1..n:
    p[0]=i
    j0=0
    minv=[inf]*(n+1)
    used=[False]*(n+1)
    while True:
        used[j0]=True
        i0=p[j0]
        delta=inf
        for j...
            cur = a[i0][j]-u[i0]-v[j]
            if cur < minv[j]: ...
        for j...
            if used[j]: u[p[j]]+=delta; v[j]-=delta
            else: minv[j]-=delta
        j0=j1
        if p[j0]==0: break
    augment
```
This updates potentials every time a column is added. It is O(n^3) with update loop each Dijkstra iteration. Could instead run Dijkstra without updating potentials until the end, using `dist` arrays:
```
dist[j] = min over previous rows of a[i0][j]-u[i0]-v[j] + dist[prev]?
```
Actually the standard algorithm's `minv` after potential updates represents the shortest distance from root to column j in the reduced cost graph. The update loop adjusts potentials so that the minimum `minv` becomes 0 for the chosen column. This is necessary to maintain nonnegative reduced costs for future rows. But we could update potentials once after finding the augmenting path using the `minv` values as distances? Let's recall another common implementation:
```
for i:
    p[0]=i
    j0=0
    minv=[inf]*(n+1)
    used=[False]*(n+1)
    while True:
        used[j0]=True
        i0=p[j0]
        delta=inf
        for j not used:
            cur = a[i0][j]-u[i0]-v[j]
            if cur < minv[j]: minv[j]=cur; way[j]=j0
            if minv[j] < delta: delta=minv[j]; j1=j
        for j in 0..n:
            if used[j]:
                u[p[j]] += delta
                v[j] -= delta
            else:
                minv[j] -= delta
        j0=j1
        if p[j0]==0: break
    augment
```
This is standard.

Alternative: We can update potentials only once per row after finding path? Let's search memory: The e-maxx algorithm updates potentials inside the loop. The "simple" O(n^3) Hungarian for rectangular uses `minv` and updates potentials in each iteration. I think update loop is needed to make `minv` for used columns zero and to maintain potentials for future. But maybe we can postpone potential update and instead use `minv` as distances; after Dijkstra, update potentials by `delta` for all visited? Let's derive.

In assignment problem, for each new row, we need to find an augmenting path in the equality graph. The standard algorithm maintains potentials such that reduced costs are nonnegative. Dijkstra computes shortest distances `minv[j]` from root to column j in the residual graph with reduced costs. When a column is chosen, we update potentials: for all visited columns/rows, u += dist[chosen], v -= dist[chosen]; for unvisited, minv -= dist[chosen]. This is exactly what the loop does, but it does it incrementally as each column is chosen. Could instead run a full Dijkstra to compute all `minv` and `way`, then update potentials once using the final `minv[chosen]` as delta? But the chosen column is the one with minimum minv; after updating potentials, that minv becomes 0 and the path is found. However, Dijkstra's distances depend on potentials, which are constant during Dijkstra if we don't update. The standard algorithm's `minv` values before potential update are the shortest distances in the current reduced cost graph. We could compute them with a priority queue or O(n^2) Dijkstra without updating potentials, then update potentials once by the distance to the newly matched column? Let's see.

Actually, the standard algorithm's inner loop is exactly Dijkstra where potentials are updated after each relaxation? Wait, it updates potentials after selecting the minimum `minv` among unvisited columns. This is equivalent to Dijkstra where the distance labels are `minv` and potentials are adjusted to keep labels nonnegative; but the `minv` values are not the actual distances? Let's test. In Dijkstra, we would:
- Initialize dist[j] = cost(root,j) - u[root] - v[j].
- Select unvisited column with min dist.
- Relax through its matched row: for each unvisited k, dist[k] = min(dist[k], dist[j] + cost(row,k)-u[row]-v[k]).
- Repeat.
No potential updates until end. The standard algorithm instead, after selecting j1 with min minv, updates potentials by delta = minv[j1], which subtracts delta from all unvisited minv. This makes the selected column's minv zero and updates u/v for visited rows/cols. This is equivalent to maintaining reduced costs relative to updated potentials, so `minv` values are distances minus delta? It is a standard optimized Dijkstra. The number of operations is similar.

Let's maybe optimize the Hungarian inner loop by using `for j in range(1, n1):` and local variables `used_list = used`, `v_list = v`, `minv_list = minv`. Already local. We can also store `n1 = n+1`.

Let's write a more optimized version:

```
def hungarian(a):
    n = len(a)
    n1 = n + 1
    u = [0.0] * n1
    v = [0.0] * n1
    p = [0] * n1
    way = [0] * n1
    for i in range(1, n1):
        p[0] = i
        j0 = 0
        minv = [inf] * n1
        used = [False] * n1
        while True:
            used[j0] = True
            i0 = p[j0]
            row = a[i0 - 1]
            ui = u[i0]
            delta = inf
            j1 = 0
            for j in range(1, n1):
                if not used[j]:
                    cur = row[j - 1] - ui - v[j]
                    if cur < minv[j]:
                        minv[j] = cur
                        way[j] = j0
                    if minv[j] < delta:
                        delta = minv[j]
                        j1 = j
            if delta < 0:
                delta = 0.0
            for j in range(n1):
                if used[j]:
                    u[p[j]] += delta
                    v[j] -= delta
                else:
                    minv[j] -= delta
            j0 = j1
            if p[j0] == 0:
                break
        while True:
            j1 = way[j0]
            p[j0] = p[j1]
            j0 = j1
            if j0 == 0:
                break
    ans = [0] * n
    for j in range(1, n1):
        ans[p[j] - 1] = j
    return ans
```

Potential issue: If `delta` is `inf`, `if delta < 0` false. If `delta` is `nan`, false. no.

Let's maybe write the final code now mentally:

```
import sys
from math import sqrt, inf

def hungarian(a):
    n = len(a)
    n1 = n + 1
    u = [0.0] * n1
    v = [0.0] * n1
    p = [0] * n1
    way = [0] * n1
    for i in range(1, n1):
        p[0] = i
        j0 = 0
        minv = [inf] * n1
        used = [False] * n1
        while True:
            used[j0] = True
            i0 = p[j0]
            row = a[i0 - 1]
            ui = u[i0]
            delta = inf
            j1 = 0
            for j in range(1, n1):
                if not used[j]:
                    cur = row[j - 1] - ui - v[j]
                    if cur < minv[j]:
                        minv[j] = cur
                        way[j] = j0
                    if minv[j] < delta:
                        delta = minv[j]
                        j1 = j
            for j in range(n1):
                if used[j]:
                    u[p[j]] += delta
                    v[j] -= delta
                else:
                    minv[j] -= delta
            j0 = j1
            if p[j0] == 0:
                break
        while True:
            j1 = way[j0]
            p[j0] = p[j1]
            j0 = j1
            if j0 == 0:
                break
    ans = [0] * n
    for j in range(1, n1):
        ans[p[j] - 1] = j - 1
    return ans

def main():
    data = sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()
    if not data:
        return
    it = iter(data)
    N = int(next(it))
    P = [(int(next(it)), int(next(it))) for _ in range(N)]
    Q = [(int(next(it)), int(next(it))) for _ in range(N)]

    cost = [[0.0] * N for _ in range(N)]
    for i in range(N):
        xi, yi = P[i]
        row = cost[i]
        for j in range(N):
            xj, yj = Q[j]
            dx = xi - xj
            dy = yi - yj
            row[j] = sqrt(dx * dx + dy * dy)

    ans = hungarian(cost)

    # Uncross any crossing pair if numerical issues made the matching cross.
    while True:
        swapped = False
        for i in range(N):
            xi, yi = P[i]
            qi = ans[i]
            xa, ya = Q[qi]
            for j in range(i + 1, N):
                xj, yj = P[j]
                qj = ans[j]
                xb, yb = Q[qj]

                o1 = (xa - xi) * (yj - ya) - (ya - yi) * (xj - xa)
                o2 = (xa - xi) * (yb - ya) - (ya - yi) * (xb - xa)
                if o1 * o2 >= 0:
                    continue
                o3 = (xb - xj) * (yi - yb) - (yb - yj) * (xi - xb)
                o4 = (xb - xj) * (ya - yb) - (yb - yj) * (xa - xb)
                if o3 * o4 < 0:
                    ans[i], ans[j] = ans[j], ans[i]
                    swapped = True
                    break
            if swapped:
                break
        if not swapped:
            break

    sys.stdout.write(' '.join(str(x + 1) for x in ans) + '\n')

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```

Let's double-check the orientation formulas in the uncrossing loop with the variables:
Segment i: A = P[i] = (xi, yi), B = Q[qi] = (xa, ya).
Segment j: C = P[j] = (xj, yj), D = Q[qj] = (xb, yb).
o1 = orient(A,B,C) = (B-A) x (C-A) = (xa-xi)*(yj-ya) - (ya-yi)*(xj-xa). Correct.
o2 = orient(A,B,D) = (xa-xi)*(yb-ya) - (ya-yi)*(xb-xa). Correct.
o3 = orient(C,D,A) = (D-C) x (A-C) = (xb-xj)*(yi-yb) - (yb-yj)*(xi-xb). Correct.
o4 = orient(C,D,B) = (xb-xj)*(ya-yb) - (yb-yj)*(xa-xb). Correct.
If o1*o2 <0 and o3*o4 <0, segments cross. Good.

Let's think about if we can reduce the update loop by combining with scan? There is a variant:
```
for j in range(n1):
    if used[j]:
        u[p[j]] += delta
        v[j] -= delta
    else:
       
</think>

=== ANSWER ===
```python
import sys
from math import sqrt, inf


def hungarian(a):
    n = len(a)
    n1 = n + 1
    u = [0.0] * n1
    v = [0.0] * n1
    p = [0] * n1
    way = [0] * n1

    for i in range(1, n1):
        p[0] = i
        j0 = 0
        minv = [inf] * n1
        used = [False] * n1

        while True:
            used[j0] = True
            i0 = p[j0]
            row = a[i0 - 1]
            ui = u[i0]
            delta = inf
            j1 = 0

            for j in range(1, n1):
                if not used[j]:
                    cur = row[j - 1] - ui - v[j]
                    if cur < minv[j]:
                        minv[j] = cur
                        way[j] = j0
                    if minv[j] < delta:
                        delta = minv[j]
                        j1 = j

            for j in range(n1):
                if used[j]:
                    u[p[j]] += delta
                    v[j] -= delta
                else:
                    minv[j] -= delta

            j0 = j1
            if p[j0] == 0:
                break

        while True:
            j1 = way[j0]
            p[j0] = p[j1]
            j0 = j1
            if j0 == 0:
                break

    ans = [0] * n
    for j in range(1, n1):
        ans[p[j] - 1] = j - 1
    return ans


def main():
    data = sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()
    if not data:
        return

    it = iter(data)
    N = int(next(it))

    P = [(int(next(it)), int(next(it))) for _ in range(N)]
    Q = [(int(next(it)), int(next(it))) for _ in range(N)]

    cost = [[0.0] * N for _ in range(N)]
    for i in range(N):
        xi, yi = P[i]
        row = cost[i]
        for j in range(N):
            xj, yj = Q[j]
            dx = xi - xj
            dy = yi - yj
            row[j] = sqrt(dx * dx + dy * dy)

    ans = hungarian(cost)

    # Safety uncrossing. The Hungarian result is already optimal and hence
    # non-crossing, but this also fixes any tiny floating-point edge case.
    while True:
        swapped = False
        for i in range(N):
            xi, yi = P[i]
            qi = ans[i]
            xa, ya = Q[qi]

            for j in range(i + 1, N):
                xj, yj = P[j]
                qj = ans[j]
                xb, yb = Q[qj]

                o1 = (xa - xi) * (yj - ya) - (ya - yi) * (xj - xa)
                o2 = (xa - xi) * (yb - ya) - (ya - yi) * (xb - xa)
                if o1 * o2 >= 0:
                    continue

                o3 = (xb - xj) * (yi - yb) - (yb - yj) * (xi - xb)
                o4 = (xb - xj) * (ya - yb) - (yb - yj) * (xa - xb)
                if o3 * o4 < 0:
                    ans[i], ans[j] = ans[j], ans[i]
                    swapped = True
                    break

            if swapped:
                break

        if not swapped:
            break

    sys.stdout.write(" ".join(str(x + 1) for x in ans) + "\n")


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```
